## Death

Main article: [Death of Michael Jackson](/wiki/Death_of_Michael_Jackson "Death of Michael Jackson")

[](/wiki/File:Michael_Jackson_Star.JPG)Fans placed flowers and notes on Jackson's star on the Hollywood Walk of Fame on the day of his death.

On June 25, 2009, less than three weeks before his concert residency was due to begin in London, with all concerts sold out, Jackson died at age 50 from cardiac arrest, caused by a [propofol](/wiki/Propofol "Propofol") and [benzodiazepine](/wiki/Benzodiazepine "Benzodiazepine") overdose.[322][323] [Conrad Murray](/wiki/Conrad_Murray "Conrad Murray"), his personal physician, had given Jackson various medications to help him sleep at his rented mansion in [Holmby Hills, Los Angeles](/wiki/Holmby_Hills,_Los_Angeles "Holmby Hills, Los Angeles"). Paramedics received a [911](/wiki/911_\(emergency_telephone_number\) "911 \(emergency telephone number\)") call at 12:21 pm [Pacific time](/wiki/Pacific_time "Pacific time") (19:21 UTC) and arrived at the property four minutes later.[324] Jackson was not breathing and [CPR](/wiki/CPR "CPR") was performed.[325] Resuscitation efforts continued en route to [Ronald Reagan UCLA Medical Center](/wiki/Ronald_Reagan_UCLA_Medical_Center "Ronald Reagan UCLA Medical Center"), and for more than an hour after Jackson's arrival, but were unsuccessful,[326][327] and he was pronounced dead at 2:26 pm Pacific time (21:26 UTC).[328][329] Murray had administered propofol, [lorazepam](/wiki/Lorazepam "Lorazepam"), and [midazolam](/wiki/Midazolam "Midazolam");[330] Jackson's death was caused by a propofol overdose.[323][327]

News of his death spread quickly online, causing websites to slow down and [crash](/wiki/Crash_\(computing\) "Crash \(computing\)") from user overload,[331] and it put unprecedented strain[332] on many services and websites including [Google](/wiki/Google_Search "Google Search"),[333] [AOL Instant Messenger](/wiki/AOL_Instant_Messenger "AOL Instant Messenger"),[332] [Twitter](/wiki/Twitter "Twitter")[333] and [Wikipedia](/wiki/Wikipedia "Wikipedia").[333] Overall, [web traffic](/wiki/Web_traffic "Web traffic") rose by between 11% and 20%.[334][335] MTV and [BET](/wiki/BET "BET") aired [marathons](/wiki/Marathon_\(media\) "Marathon \(media\)") of Jackson's music videos,[336] and Jackson specials aired on television stations around the world.[337] MTV briefly returned to its original music video format,[11] and they aired hours of Jackson's music videos, with live news specials featuring reactions from MTV personalities and other celebrities.[338] President [Barack Obama](/wiki/Barack_Obama "Barack Obama") sent a letter of condolence to the Jackson family,[339] and the [House of Representatives](/wiki/United_States_House_of_Representatives "United States House of Representatives") observed a [moment of silence](/wiki/Moment_of_silence "Moment of silence").[340]

### Memorial service

Main article: [Michael Jackson memorial service](/wiki/Michael_Jackson_memorial_service "Michael Jackson memorial service")

[](/wiki/File:Michael_Jackson_Grave.jpg)

Jackson's unmarked crypt at the end of the Sanctuary of Ascension in the Holly Terrace of the [Great Mausoleum, Forest Lawn Glendale](/wiki/Forest_Lawn_Memorial_Park_\(Glendale\) "Forest Lawn Memorial Park \(Glendale\)")

[](/wiki/File:Neverland-ranch-memorial.jpg)

Fans visiting the makeshift memorial set up outside the Neverland Ranch entrance shortly after Jackson's death

Jackson's memorial was held on July 7, 2009, at the [Staples Center](/wiki/Staples_Center "Staples Center") in Los Angeles, preceded by a private family service at [Forest Lawn Memorial Park's Hall of Liberty](/wiki/Forest_Lawn_Memorial_Park,_Hollywood_Hills#Court_of_Liberty "Forest Lawn Memorial Park, Hollywood Hills"). Over 1.6 million fans applied for tickets to the memorial; the 8,750 recipients were drawn at random, and each received two tickets.[341] The memorial service was one of the most watched events in [streaming](/wiki/Streaming "Streaming") history,[342] with an estimated US audience of 31.1 million[343] and a worldwide audience of an estimated 2.5 to 3 billion.[344][345]

[Mariah Carey](/wiki/Mariah_Carey "Mariah Carey"), [Stevie Wonder](/wiki/Stevie_Wonder "Stevie Wonder"), [Lionel Richie](/wiki/Lionel_Richie "Lionel Richie"), [Jennifer Hudson](/wiki/Jennifer_Hudson "Jennifer Hudson"), and [Shaheen Jafargholi](/wiki/Shaheen_Jafargholi "Shaheen Jafargholi") performed at the memorial, and [Berry Gordy](/wiki/Berry_Gordy "Berry Gordy"), [Brooke Shields](/wiki/Brooke_Shields "Brooke Shields"), [Smokey Robinson](/wiki/Smokey_Robinson "Smokey Robinson") and [Queen Latifah](/wiki/Queen_Latifah "Queen Latifah") gave eulogies.[346] [Al Sharpton](/wiki/Al_Sharpton "Al Sharpton") received a standing ovation with cheers when he told Jackson's children: "Wasn't nothing strange about your daddy. It was strange what your daddy had to deal with. But he dealt with it anyway."[347] Jackson's 11-year-old daughter [Paris Katherine](/wiki/Paris_Jackson "Paris Jackson"), speaking publicly for the first time, wept as she addressed the crowd.[348][349] Lucious Smith provided a closing prayer.[350] On September 3, 2009, the body of Jackson was entombed at [Forest Lawn Memorial Park](/wiki/Forest_Lawn_Memorial_Park_\(Glendale\) "Forest Lawn Memorial Park \(Glendale\)") in [Glendale, California](/wiki/Glendale,_California "Glendale, California").[351]

### Criminal investigation and prosecution of Conrad Murray

Main article: [_People v. Murray_](/wiki/People_v._Murray "People v. Murray")

In August 2009, the [Los Angeles County Coroner](/wiki/Los_Angeles_County_Coroner "Los Angeles County Coroner") ruled that Jackson's death was a [homicide](/wiki/Homicide "Homicide").[352][353] Law enforcement officials charged Murray with [involuntary manslaughter](/wiki/Involuntary_manslaughter "Involuntary manslaughter") on February 8, 2010.[354] In late 2011, he was found guilty of said charge[355] and held without bail to await sentencing.[356] Murray was sentenced to four years in prison, but was released after one year and eleven months.[357]

### Posthumous sales

At the [2009 American Music Awards](/wiki/2009_American_Music_Awards "2009 American Music Awards"), Jackson won four posthumous awards, including two for his compilation album _[Number Ones](/wiki/Number_Ones_\(Michael_Jackson_album\) "Number Ones \(Michael Jackson album\)")_ , bringing his total American Music Awards to 26.[358][359] In the year after his death, more than 16.1 million copies of Jackson's albums were sold in the US alone, and 35 million copies were sold worldwide, more than any other artist in 2009.[360][361] He became the first artist to sell one million [music downloads](/wiki/Music_download "Music download") in a week, with 2.6 million song downloads. _Thriller_ , _Number Ones_ and _[The Essential Michael Jackson](/wiki/The_Essential_Michael_Jackson "The Essential Michael Jackson")_ became the first catalog albums to outsell any new album.[362] Jackson also became the first artist to have four of the top-20 best-selling albums in a single year in the US.[363] Within the year following his death, Jackson sold over 75 million records worldwide.[364] By the end of 2013, Jackson had sold over 50 million albums worldwide since his death.[365]

Following the surge in sales, in March 2010, Sony Music signed a $250 million deal (equivalent to $370 million in 2025) with the [Jackson estate](/wiki/Jackson_estate "Jackson estate") to extend their distribution rights to Jackson's back catalog until at least 2017; it had been due to expire in 2015. It was the [most expensive music contract](/wiki/List_of_largest_music_deals "List of largest music deals") for a single artist in history.[366][367] They agreed to release ten albums of previously unreleased material and new collections of released work.[366][368] The deal was extended in 2017.[369] That July, a Los Angeles court awarded [Quincy Jones](/wiki/Quincy_Jones "Quincy Jones") $9.4 million of disputed royalty payments for _Off the Wall_ , _Thriller_ , and _Bad_.[53] In July 2018, Sony/ATV bought the estate's stake in EMI for $287.5 million.[370]

In 2015, _Thriller_ became the first album to be certified for 30 million shipments by the [Recording Industry Association of America](/wiki/Recording_Industry_Association_of_America "Recording Industry Association of America") (RIAA).[371] A year later, it was certified 33× platinum after [Soundscan](/wiki/Soundscan "Soundscan") added streams and audio downloads to album certifications.[372][nb 3]

In February 2024, Sony Music acquired half of Jackson's publishing rights and recording masters for $600 million. The deal includes assets from his [Mijac publishing catalog](/wiki/Mijac_Music "Mijac Music"), but excludes royalties from several Jackson-related productions, such as the _MJ_ Broadway musical and the 2026 _[Michael](/wiki/Michael_\(2026_film\) "Michael \(2026 film\)")_ film. The transaction is possibly the largest for a single musician's work.[374][375]

### Posthumous releases and productions

Jackson's posthumous releases and productions are administered by the [estate of Michael Jackson](/wiki/Estate_of_Michael_Jackson "Estate of Michael Jackson"), which owns Jackson's trademarks and rights to his name, image and likeness.[376] The first posthumous Jackson song, "[This Is It](/wiki/This_Is_It_\(Michael_Jackson_song\) "This Is It \(Michael Jackson song\)")", co-written in the 1980s with [Paul Anka](/wiki/Paul_Anka "Paul Anka"), was released in October 2009. The surviving Jackson brothers reunited to record backing vocals.[377] It was followed by a documentary film about the rehearsals for the canceled This Is It tour, _[Michael Jackson's This Is It](/wiki/Michael_Jackson%27s_This_Is_It "Michael Jackson's This Is It")_ ,[378] and [a compilation album](/wiki/Michael_Jackson%27s_This_Is_It_\(album\) "Michael Jackson's This Is It \(album\)").[379] Despite a limited two-week engagement, the film became the highest-grossing documentary or concert film ever, with earnings of more than $260 million worldwide.[380] Jackson's estate received 90% of the profits.[381] In late 2010, Sony released the first posthumous album, _[Michael](/wiki/Michael_\(Michael_Jackson_album\) "Michael \(Michael Jackson album\)")_ , and the lead single "[Hold My Hand](/wiki/Hold_My_Hand_\(Michael_Jackson_and_Akon_song\) "Hold My Hand \(Michael Jackson and Akon song\)")", a duet with [Akon](/wiki/Akon "Akon"). The Jackson collaborator will.i.am expressed disgust, saying that Jackson would not have approved the release.[382]

The video game developer [Ubisoft](/wiki/Ubisoft "Ubisoft") released a [music game](/wiki/Music_game "Music game") featuring Jackson for the 2010 holiday season, _[Michael Jackson: The Experience](/wiki/Michael_Jackson:_The_Experience "Michael Jackson: The Experience")_. It was among the first games to use [Kinect](/wiki/Kinect "Kinect") and [PlayStation Move](/wiki/PlayStation_Move "PlayStation Move"), the motion-detecting camera systems for [Xbox 360](/wiki/Xbox_360 "Xbox 360") and [PlayStation 3](/wiki/PlayStation_3 "PlayStation 3").[383] In October 2011, the theater company [Cirque du Soleil](/wiki/Cirque_du_Soleil "Cirque du Soleil") launched _[Michael Jackson: The Immortal World Tour](/wiki/Michael_Jackson:_The_Immortal_World_Tour "Michael Jackson: The Immortal World Tour")_ , a $57 million production in Montreal.[384] A larger and more theatrical Cirque show, _[Michael Jackson: One](/wiki/Michael_Jackson:_One "Michael Jackson: One")_ , designed for residency at the [Mandalay Bay](/wiki/Mandalay_Bay "Mandalay Bay") resort in Las Vegas, opened on May 23, 2013, in a renovated theater.[385][386]

In 2012, in an attempt to end a family dispute, Jackson's brother Jermaine retracted his signature on a public letter criticizing executors of Jackson's estate and his mother's advisors over the legitimacy of his brother's will.[387] T.J. Jackson, the son of Tito Jackson, was given co-guardianship of Michael Jackson's children after false reports of Katherine Jackson going missing.[388] _[Xscape](/wiki/Xscape_\(album\) "Xscape \(album\)")_ , an album of unreleased material, was released on May 13, 2014.[389] The lead single, a duet between Jackson and [Justin Timberlake](/wiki/Justin_Timberlake "Justin Timberlake"), "[Love Never Felt So Good](/wiki/Love_Never_Felt_So_Good "Love Never Felt So Good")", reached number 9 on the US _Billboard_ Hot 100, making Jackson the first artist to have a top-10 single on the chart in five different decades.[390]

Later in 2014, [Queen](/wiki/Queen_\(band\) "Queen \(band\)") released a duet recorded with Jackson in the 1980s.[65] A compilation album, _[Scream](/wiki/Scream_\(Michael_Jackson_album\) "Scream \(Michael Jackson album\)")_ , was released on September 29, 2017.[391] A [jukebox musical](/wiki/Jukebox_musical "Jukebox musical"), _[MJ the Musical](/wiki/MJ_the_Musical "MJ the Musical")_ , premiered on Broadway in 2022.[392] [Myles Frost](/wiki/Myles_Frost "Myles Frost") won the 2022 [Tony Award for Best Actor in a Musical](/wiki/Tony_Award_for_Best_Actor_in_a_Musical "Tony Award for Best Actor in a Musical") for his portrayal of Jackson.[393] On November 18, 2022, Epic released a [40th-anniversary edition reissue of _Thriller_](/wiki/Thriller_40 "Thriller 40").[394][395] Since Jackson's death, his estate has grossed $2 billion in ticket revenue from _MJ the Musical_ , _[Michael Jackson's This Is It](/wiki/Michael_Jackson%27s_This_Is_It "Michael Jackson's This Is It")_ and two Cirque du Soleil productions.[396] A biographical film, _[Michael](/wiki/Michael_\(2026_film\) "Michael \(2026 film\)")_ , directed by [Antoine Fuqua](/wiki/Antoine_Fuqua "Antoine Fuqua") and starring Jackson's nephew [Jaafar Jackson](/wiki/Jaafar_Jackson "Jaafar Jackson"), premiered in April 2026.[397] It received generally negative reviews from critics, but positive reception from audiences.[398][399] [400] The film broke several records for biopics on opening day and opening week both domestically and internationally, with an opening weekend gross estimated at $97 million domestically and $217 million worldwide, becoming [the sixth highest-grossing film of 2026](/wiki/2026_in_film#Highest-grossing_films "2026 in film").[401]

### Posthumous child sexual abuse allegations

In 2013, choreographer [Wade Robson](/wiki/Wade_Robson "Wade Robson") filed a lawsuit alleging that Jackson had sexually abused him for seven years, beginning when he was seven years old (1989–1996).[402] In 2014, a case was filed by James Safechuck, alleging similar sexual abuse over a four-year period starting when Safechuck was ten (1988–1992).[403][404][405] Both had previously testified in Jackson's defense during the 1993 allegations; Robson did so again in 2005.[406][407] In 2015, Robson's case against Jackson's estate was dismissed as it had been filed too late. Safechuck's claim was also time-barred.[408]

In 2017, it was ruled that Jackson's corporations could not be held accountable for his alleged past actions.[409][410] The rulings were appealed. On October 20, 2020, Safechuck's lawsuit against Jackson's corporations was again dismissed. The judge ruled that there was no evidence that Safechuck had a relationship with Jackson's corporation, nor was it proven that there was a special relationship between the two.[411][412][413][414] On April 26, 2021, Robson's case was dismissed because of a lack of supporting evidence that the defendants exercised control over Jackson.[415]

Robson and Safechuck's allegations were the subject of the documentary film _[Leaving Neverland](/wiki/Leaving_Neverland "Leaving Neverland")_ , broadcast by HBO in March 2019.[416] Radio stations in New Zealand, Canada, the UK and the Netherlands removed Jackson's music from their playlists.[417][418][419] Jackson's family condemned the film as a "public lynching",[420] and the Jackson estate released a statement calling the film a "tabloid character assassination [Jackson] endured in life, and now in death".[421] Close associates of Jackson, such as [Corey Feldman](/wiki/Corey_Feldman "Corey Feldman"), [Aaron Carter](/wiki/Aaron_Carter "Aaron Carter"), Brett Barnes, and [Macaulay Culkin](/wiki/Macaulay_Culkin "Macaulay Culkin"), defended Jackson in the wake of the documentary's release, saying that Jackson had never molested them.[422][423][424]

Documentaries such as _[Square One: Michael Jackson](/wiki/Square_One:_Michael_Jackson "Square One: Michael Jackson")_ , _[Neverland Firsthand: Investigating the Michael Jackson Documentary](/wiki/Neverland_Firsthand:_Investigating_the_Michael_Jackson_Documentary "Neverland Firsthand: Investigating the Michael Jackson Documentary")_ and _[Michael Jackson: Chase the Truth](/wiki/Michael_Jackson:_Chase_the_Truth "Michael Jackson: Chase the Truth")_ , presented information countering the claims suggested by _Leaving Neverland_.[425][426][427] Jackson's album sales increased following the documentary screenings.[428] _Billboard_ senior editor Gail Mitchell said she and a colleague interviewed about thirty music executives who believed Jackson's legacy could withstand the controversy.[429] In late 2019, some New Zealand and Canadian radio stations re-added Jackson's music to their playlists due to "positive listener survey results".[430][431]

On February 21, 2019, the Jackson estate sued HBO for breaching a non-disparagement clause from a 1992 contract. The suit sought to compel HBO to participate in a non-confidential arbitration that could result in $100 million or more in damages awarded to the estate.[432] HBO said they did not breach a contract and filed an anti-[SLAPP](/wiki/SLAPP "SLAPP") motion against the estate. In September 2019, Judge [George H. Wu](/wiki/George_H._Wu "George H. Wu") denied HBO's motion to dismiss the case, allowing the Jackson estate to arbitrate.[433] HBO appealed, but in December 2020 the appeals court affirmed Wu's ruling.[434] In 2024, the Jackson estate and HBO reached a settlement; as part of the settlement, HBO removed _Leaving Neverland_ from the [HBO Max](/wiki/HBO_Max "HBO Max") streaming service.[435]

In 2020, a state law passed in California which granted plaintiffs in child sex abuse cases an additional period to file lawsuits. In October 2020 and again in April 2021, the [Los Angeles County Superior Court](/wiki/Los_Angeles_County_Superior_Court "Los Angeles County Superior Court") ruled in Safechuck v. MJJ Productions that MJJ Productions Inc. and MJJ Ventures Inc. employees were not legally obligated to protect the two men from Jackson. In August 2023, California's Second District Court of Appeal overturned the ruling, and the case was approved to move forward to [trial court](/wiki/Trial_court "Trial court").[436]

In 2026, three male (Aldo, Frank, and Eddie) and one female (Marie Nicole) adult siblings of the Cascio family filed a federal lawsuit against Jackson's estate alleging sexual abuse that spanned from childhood to adulthood. The lawsuit followed a trial court's ruling to grant the estate's petition to arbitrate against Frank Cascio where he was being sued for civil extortion after a $213 million demand and threats to go public with allegations of abuse.[437][438][439]

## Legacy

Main article: [Cultural impact of Michael Jackson](/wiki/Cultural_impact_of_Michael_Jackson "Cultural impact of Michael Jackson")

See also: [List of Michael Jackson records and achievements](/wiki/List_of_Michael_Jackson_records_and_achievements "List of Michael Jackson records and achievements")

[](/wiki/File:Michael_Jackson_with_the_Reagans.jpg)Jackson at the [White House](/wiki/White_House "White House") in 1984. President [Ronald Reagan](/wiki/Ronald_Reagan "Ronald Reagan") described him as "one of the most talented, most popular and most exciting superstars".[440]

Jackson has been referred to as the "[King of Pop](/wiki/Honorific_nicknames_in_popular_music "Honorific nicknames in popular music")" for having transformed the art of music videos and paving the way for modern pop music. For much of Jackson's career, he had an unparalleled worldwide influence over the younger generation.[441] His influence extended beyond the music industry; he impacted dance, led fashion trends, and raised awareness for global affairs.[442] Jackson's music and videos fostered racial diversity in MTV's roster and steered its focus from rock to pop music and R&B, leading to the discontinuation of the [album-oriented rock](/wiki/Album-oriented_rock "Album-oriented rock") format previously dominant on the channel.[40][443] In songs such as "[Black or White](/wiki/Black_or_White "Black or White")", "[Heal the World](/wiki/Heal_the_World "Heal the World")", "[Earth Song](/wiki/Earth_Song "Earth Song")" and "[They Don't Care About Us](/wiki/They_Don%27t_Care_About_Us "They Don't Care About Us")", Jackson's music emphasized [racial integration](/wiki/Racial_integration "Racial integration") and [environmentalism](/wiki/Environmentalism "Environmentalism") and protested injustice.[444][445] He is considered one of the most significant cultural figures of the 20th century,[446] and his contributions to music, dance, and fashion, along with his publicized personal life, made him a global figure in popular culture for over four decades.[447][448][449][450]

Jackson's meteoric rise in the 1980s catapulted him to global stardom, and his influence transcended borders. In Africa, his influence was compared to [Nelson Mandela](/wiki/Nelson_Mandela "Nelson Mandela") for his ability to inspire and unite diverse audiences through his music.[451][452] Similarly, in India, Jackson was likened to [Mahatma Gandhi](/wiki/Mahatma_Gandhi "Mahatma Gandhi"), with his art championing themes of social justice and [humanitarianism](/wiki/Humanitarianism "Humanitarianism"),[453] and his influence extending to [Bollywood](/wiki/Bollywood "Bollywood") where it was said that anyone who danced well was compared to Jackson.[451] In Europe, Jackson's impact was compared to that of the [Pope](/wiki/Pope "Pope"); huge crowds gathered to see him in public and even more attended his concerts, which spread messages of love and healing during rough economic and political times.[454] His presence in the Middle East was considered as widespread as [Coca-Cola](/wiki/Coca-Cola "Coca-Cola"), symbolizing a shared global culture through his music.[455] Additionally, Jackson is considered the backbone of the [K-pop](/wiki/K-pop "K-pop") industry in South Korea, influencing countless artists and shaping the genre's development.[456][457]

> So vast, far-reaching and was his impact—particularly in the wake of _[Thriller](/wiki/Thriller_\(album\) "Thriller \(album\)")_ 's colossal and heretofore unmatched commercial success—that there weren't a whole lot of artists who _weren't_ trying to mimic some of the Jackson formula.

— J. Edward Keyes of _[Rolling Stone](/wiki/Rolling_Stone "Rolling Stone")_[458]

[Danyel Smith](/wiki/Danyel_Smith "Danyel Smith"), chief content officer of Vibe Media Group and the editor-in-chief of _[Vibe](/wiki/Vibe_\(magazine\) "Vibe \(magazine\)")_ , described Jackson as "the greatest star".[459] Steve Huey of AllMusic called him "an unstoppable juggernaut, possessed of all the skills to dominate the charts seemingly at will: an instantly identifiable voice, eye-popping dance moves, stunning musical versatility and loads of sheer star power".[10] BET said Jackson was "quite simply the greatest entertainer of all time" whose "sound, style, movement and legacy continues to inspire artists of all genres".[460] In 1984, _[Time](/wiki/Time_\(magazine\) "Time \(magazine\)")_ pop critic [Jay Cocks](/wiki/Jay_Cocks "Jay Cocks") wrote that Jackson was the biggest phenomenon since the Beatles and Elvis Presley, while possibly "the most popular black singer ever". Cocks declared him a "star of records, radio, rock video" and the "dancer with the fanciest feet".[85] In 2003, _[The Daily Telegraph](/wiki/The_Daily_Telegraph "The Daily Telegraph")_ writer [Tom Utley](/wiki/Tom_Utley "Tom Utley") described Jackson as "extremely important" and a "genius".[461] At Jackson's memorial service on July 7, 2009, Motown founder Berry Gordy called Jackson "the greatest entertainer that ever lived".[462][463] Jackson is recognized as the "Most Successful Entertainer of All Time" by _[Guinness World Records](/wiki/Guinness_World_Records "Guinness World Records")_.[464][465] He has also appeared on _Rolling Stone_ 's lists of the "Greatest Singers of All Time".[466][467]

Craig Glenday, the editor-in-chief of _Guinness World Records_ , called Jackson the most famous person in the world in 2006. Following Jackson's death, Glenday wrote in an obituary that Jackson had maintained this status up until his death,[468] later remarking that his fame had exceeded that of [Confucius](/wiki/Confucius "Confucius").[469] _[The Guardian](/wiki/The_Guardian "The Guardian")_ wrote that he was in a league of his own in terms of fame, noting that Jackson had become so famous that the number of people who might not know who he was had become statistically insignificant.[470] Due to his unprecedented influence, Jackson is widely recognized as one of the most famous and globally renowned figures in history.[471] Reports of his fame extend from the Middle East,[472][473] Africa,[451][474] India,[475][476] and China[477][478] to tribes in the Amazon.[479] His influence even reached remote corners of the world such as [São Tomé and Príncipe](/wiki/S%C3%A3o_Tom%C3%A9_and_Pr%C3%ADncipe "São Tomé and Príncipe"), or [Tristan da Cunha](/wiki/Tristan_da_Cunha "Tristan da Cunha"), where tribute artists keep his legacy alive by celebrating his music.[480][481]

[](/wiki/File:Michael_Jackson_Madame_Tussauds_London.jpg)The Jackson wax figure at [Madame Tussauds](/wiki/Madame_Tussauds "Madame Tussauds") in London

In 2018, the [National Portrait Gallery](/wiki/National_Portrait_Gallery,_London "National Portrait Gallery, London") named Jackson the most depicted cultural figure of the century,[482] later stating that Jackson's influence on art rivaled that of [Jesus Christ](/wiki/Jesus_Christ "Jesus Christ").[483] [Nicholas Cullinan](/wiki/Nicholas_Cullinan "Nicholas Cullinan"), director of the National Portrait Gallery and curator of the _[Michael Jackson: On the Wall](/wiki/Michael_Jackson:_On_the_Wall "Michael Jackson: On the Wall")_ exhibition, described Jackson's impact on art as unprecedented and claimed in 2018 that Jackson was the most depicted figure in the history of [contemporary art](/wiki/Contemporary_art "Contemporary art").[484] In 2014, a comprehensive study conducted by researcher Young-Ho Eom at the [University of Toulouse](/wiki/University_of_Toulouse "University of Toulouse") identified Jackson as one of the most influential people of all time.[485] The study utilized advanced ranking methods, including 2D Rank and PageRank algorithms, to analyze the impact of historical figures. Jackson was prominently placed on the list of top influencers, alongside Swedish botanist [Carl Linnaeus](/wiki/Carl_Linnaeus "Carl Linnaeus"), [Adolf Hitler](/wiki/Adolf_Hitler "Adolf Hitler"), and Jesus Christ. Another study conducted in 2013 also identified Jackson as one of the most influential people of all time. This study ranked Jackson at the top of the list, alongside [Napoleon Bonaparte](/wiki/Napoleon_Bonaparte "Napoleon Bonaparte"), highlighting the extraordinary influence and global recognition that Jackson achieved throughout his career.[486] Numerous publications and academic studies[487] have noted his impact beyond music in fields such as psychology, law, chemistry, and engineering.[488][489][490]

## Philanthropy

Main article: [Philanthropy of Michael Jackson](/wiki/Philanthropy_of_Michael_Jackson "Philanthropy of Michael Jackson")

President [Ronald Reagan](/wiki/Ronald_Reagan "Ronald Reagan") rewarding Jackson in 1984 for his support of alcohol and drug abuse charities

Jackson is well known for his prolific philanthropy and humanitarianism.[491][492][493][93] Jackson's early charitable work has been described by _[The Chronicle of Philanthropy](/wiki/The_Chronicle_of_Philanthropy "The Chronicle of Philanthropy")_ as having "paved the way for the current surge in celebrity philanthropy",[494] and by the _[Los Angeles Times](/wiki/Los_Angeles_Times "Los Angeles Times")_ as having "set the standard for generosity for other entertainers".[491] By some estimates, he donated over $500 million, not accounting for inflation, to various charities over the course of his life.[491] In 1992, Jackson established his [Heal the World Foundation](/wiki/Heal_the_World_Foundation "Heal the World Foundation"), to which he donated several million dollars in revenue from his [Dangerous World Tour](/wiki/Dangerous_World_Tour "Dangerous World Tour").[495]

Jackson's philanthropic activities went beyond just monetary donations. He also performed at [benefit concerts](/wiki/Benefit_concert "Benefit concert"), some of which he arranged.[496] He gifted tickets for his regular concert performances to groups that assist underprivileged children.[497] Jackson visited sick children in hospitals around the world.[498] He donated valuable, personal and professional paraphernalia for numerous charity auctions. He received various awards and accolades for his philanthropic work, including two bestowed by American presidents. The vast breadth of Jackson's philanthropic work has earned recognition in the _[Guinness World Records](/wiki/Guinness_World_Records "Guinness World Records")_.[491][499][500]

On May 14, 1984, President [Ronald Reagan](/wiki/Ronald_Reagan "Ronald Reagan") gave Jackson an award recognizing his support of alcohol and drug abuse charities,[501] and in recognition of his support for the [Ad Council](/wiki/Ad_Council "Ad Council")'s and the [National Highway Traffic Safety Administration](/wiki/National_Highway_Traffic_Safety_Administration "National Highway Traffic Safety Administration")'s Drunk Driving Prevention campaign. Jackson allowed the campaign to use "Beat It" for its public service announcements.[502]

## Artistry

### Vocal style

[](/wiki/File:Michael_Jackson_in_1988.jpg)Jackson performing in [Vienna](/wiki/Vienna "Vienna"), Austria, 1988

Jackson was known as an expressive vocalist with [absolute pitch](/wiki/Absolute_pitch "Absolute pitch").[503][504][505] Critics describe his vocal theatricality as having a range from clear and soft to harsh and aggressive,[506][507][508][509] depending on the genre sung. Principally in history, Jackson is the sole recipient to receive [Grammy Awards](/wiki/Grammy_Awards "Grammy Awards") for three different genres of vocal performance (as a soloist), winning [Pop Vocal](/wiki/Grammy_Award_for_Best_Male_Pop_Vocal_Performance "Grammy Award for Best Male Pop Vocal Performance") for "[Thriller](/wiki/Thriller_\(song\) "Thriller \(song\)")", [Rock Vocal](/wiki/Grammy_Award_for_Best_Male_Rock_Vocal_Performance "Grammy Award for Best Male Rock Vocal Performance") for "[Beat It](/wiki/Beat_It "Beat It")", and [Rhythm and Blues Vocal](/wiki/Grammy_Award_for_Best_Male_R%26B_Vocal_Performance "Grammy Award for Best Male R&B Vocal Performance") with both "[Billie Jean](/wiki/Billie_Jean "Billie Jean")" and "[Don't Stop 'Til You Get Enough](/wiki/Don%27t_Stop_%27Til_You_Get_Enough "Don't Stop 'Til You Get Enough")". [Bruce Swedien](/wiki/Bruce_Swedien "Bruce Swedien"), his long-time sound engineer, emphasized that a critical element of their vocal recordings are of him and Jackson trying numerous approaches to rhythmic, emotional and technical distinctions to consummate a "sonic character".[510] His stylings, such as common use of [staccato](/wiki/Staccato "Staccato"), [legato](/wiki/Legato "Legato"), [falsetto](/wiki/Falsetto "Falsetto") as well as [vocal hiccups](/wiki/Vocal_hiccup "Vocal hiccup"),[511] adlibs, wailings and growls, are all signature to his sound.[512][513]

Jackson sang from childhood, and over time his voice and vocal style changed. Between 1971 and 1975, it descended from boy [soprano](/wiki/Soprano "Soprano") to lyric [tenor](/wiki/Tenor "Tenor").[514] He was known for his intonation and vocal range.[466] Through each music release, his vocal development and changes were positively narrated by music journalists. With _Off the Wall_ in the late 1970s, Jackson's abilities in his [coming-of-age](/wiki/Coming-of-age "Coming-of-age") period had _Rolling Stone_ comparing his vocals to the "breathless, dreamy stutter" of Stevie Wonder, and wrote that "Jackson's feathery-timbred tenor [was] extraordinarily beautiful. It slides smoothly into a startling [falsetto](/wiki/Falsetto "Falsetto") that's used very daringly."[515] When _Thriller_ was released in the early 1980s, _Rolling Stone_ wrote that Jackson was singing in a "fully adult voice" that was "tinged by sadness".[516] With the release of the introspective album _Dangerous_ , _The New York Times_ wrote that on some tracks, "he gulps for breath, his voice quivers with anxiety or drops to a desperate whisper, hissing through clenched teeth" and he had a "wretched tone". When singing of brotherhood or self-esteem the musician would return to "smooth" vocals.[517] Of _Invincible_ , _Rolling Stone_ wrote that, even at age 43, Jackson still performed "exquisitely voiced rhythm tracks and vibrating vocal harmonies".[518] [Joseph Vogel](/wiki/Joseph_Vogel_\(author\) "Joseph Vogel \(author\)") noted Jackson's ability to use non-verbal sounds to express emotion.[519] [Neil McCormick](/wiki/Neil_McCormick "Neil McCormick") wrote that Jackson's unorthodox singing style "was original and utterly distinctive".[520]

### Influences

Jackson was influenced by musicians including [James Brown](/wiki/James_Brown "James Brown"), [Little Richard](/wiki/Little_Richard "Little Richard"), [Jackie Wilson](/wiki/Jackie_Wilson "Jackie Wilson"), [Diana Ross](/wiki/Diana_Ross "Diana Ross"), [Fred Astaire](/wiki/Fred_Astaire "Fred Astaire"), [Sammy Davis Jr](/wiki/Sammy_Davis_Jr "Sammy Davis Jr")., [Gene Kelly](/wiki/Gene_Kelly "Gene Kelly"),[521] and [David Ruffin](/wiki/David_Ruffin "David Ruffin").[522] Little Richard had a substantial influence on Jackson,[523] but Brown was his greatest inspiration. When Jackson was a small child, his mother would wake him whenever Brown appeared on television. Jackson described being "mesmerized".[524]

Jackson's vocal technique was influenced by Diana Ross; his use of the _oooh_ interjection from a young age was something Ross had used on many of her songs with [the Supremes](/wiki/The_Supremes "The Supremes").[525] She was a mother figure to him, and he often watched her rehearse.[526] He said he had learned a lot from watching how she moved and sang, and that she had encouraged him to have confidence in himself.[527]

Choreographer [David Winters](/wiki/David_Winters_\(choreographer\) "David Winters \(choreographer\)"), who met Jackson while choreographing the 1971 Diana Ross TV special _[Diana!](/wiki/Diana! "Diana!")_ , said that Jackson watched the musical _[West Side Story](/wiki/West_Side_Story_\(1961_film\) "West Side Story \(1961 film\)")_ almost every week, and it was his favorite film; he paid tribute to it in "Beat It" and the "Bad" video.[528][529][530]

### Musicianship

Jackson had no formal music training and could not read or write [music notation](/wiki/Music_notation "Music notation"). He is credited for playing guitar, keyboard, and drums, but was not proficient in them.[531] When composing, he recorded ideas by [beatboxing](/wiki/Beatboxing "Beatboxing") and imitating instruments vocally.[531] Describing the process, he said: "I'll just sing the bass part into the [tape recorder](/wiki/Tape_recorder "Tape recorder"). I'll take that bass lick and put the chords of the melody over the bass lick and that's what inspires the melody." These were shown in his demos for "Beat It" and "Billie Jean" where he vocalized the melody, bass instrumentals as well as lead and backing vocals by overlaying each component. The engineer Robert Hoffman recalled that after Jackson came in with a song he had written overnight, Jackson sang every note of every chord to a guitar player. Hoffman also remembered Jackson singing string arrangements part by part into a cassette recorder.[531]

### Dance

Jackson danced from a young age as part of the Jackson 5,[532] and incorporated dance extensively in his performances.[532] According to Sanjoy Roy of _[The Guardian](/wiki/The_Guardian "The Guardian")_ , Jackson would "flick and retract his limbs like switchblades, or snap out of a tornado spin into a perfectly poised toe-stand".[532] The [moonwalk](/wiki/Moonwalk_\(dance\) "Moonwalk \(dance\)"), taught to him by [Jeffrey Daniel](/wiki/Jeffrey_Daniel "Jeffrey Daniel"),[75] was Jackson's signature dance move and one of the most famous of the 20th century.[533] Jackson is credited for coining the name "moonwalk" for this street dance move, previously known as the "backslide".[534][535] His other moves included the [robot](/wiki/Robot_\(dance\) "Robot \(dance\)"),[46][536] crotch grab, and the "anti-gravity" lean of the "Smooth Criminal" video.[532]

### Themes and genres

[](/wiki/File:Michael_Jackson_\(20589951783\)_\(cropped\).jpg)Jackson performing during the Dangerous World Tour in 1992

Jackson explored genres including pop,[10][537] [soul](/wiki/Soul_music "Soul music"),[10][156] [rhythm and blues](/wiki/Rhythm_and_blues "Rhythm and blues"),[537] [funk](/wiki/Funk "Funk"),[538] [rock](/wiki/Rock_music "Rock music"),[537][538] [disco](/wiki/Disco "Disco"),[539] [post-disco](/wiki/Post-disco "Post-disco"),[538] [dance-pop](/wiki/Dance-pop "Dance-pop"),[540] and [new jack swing](/wiki/New_jack_swing "New jack swing").[10] Steve Huey of [AllMusic](/wiki/AllMusic "AllMusic") wrote that _Thriller_ refined the strengths of _Off the Wall_ ; the dance and rock tracks were more aggressive, while the pop tunes and ballads were softer and more soulful.[10] Its tracks included the ballads "The Lady in My Life", "[Human Nature](/wiki/Human_Nature_\(Michael_Jackson_song\) "Human Nature \(Michael Jackson song\)")", and "[The Girl Is Mine](/wiki/The_Girl_Is_Mine "The Girl Is Mine")",[516][541][542] the funk pieces "Billie Jean" and "Wanna Be Startin' Somethin'",[516][541] and the disco set "[Baby Be Mine](/wiki/Baby_Be_Mine_\(Michael_Jackson_song\) "Baby Be Mine \(Michael Jackson song\)")" and "[P.Y.T. (Pretty Young Thing)](/wiki/P.Y.T._\(Pretty_Young_Thing\) "P.Y.T. \(Pretty Young Thing\)")".[542]

With _Off the Wall_ , _The Guardian_ 's Tom Ewing wrote that Jackson's "vocal tics – the gasps and shudders that punctuate almost every song" evoke "a singer desperate to cut loose and express himself in movement."[512] Christopher Connelly of _Rolling Stone_ commented that, with _Thriller_ , Jackson developed his long association with the subliminal theme of [paranoia](/wiki/Paranoia "Paranoia") and darker imagery.[516] AllMusic's [Stephen Thomas Erlewine](/wiki/Stephen_Thomas_Erlewine "Stephen Thomas Erlewine") noted this on the songs "Billie Jean" and "Wanna Be Startin' Somethin'".[541] In "Billie Jean", Jackson depicts an obsessive fan who alleges he has fathered her child,[10] and in "Wanna Be Startin' Somethin'" he argues against gossip and the media.[516] "Beat It" decried gang violence in a homage to _[West Side Story](/wiki/West_Side_Story "West Side Story")_ , and was Jackson's first successful rock cross-over piece, according to Huey.[10][37] He observed that "[Thriller](/wiki/Thriller_\(song\) "Thriller \(song\)")" began Jackson's interest with the theme of the [supernatural](/wiki/Supernatural "Supernatural"), a topic he revisited in subsequent years. In 1985, Jackson co-wrote the charity anthem "We Are the World"; humanitarian themes later became a recurring theme in his lyrics and public persona.[10]

In _Bad_ , Jackson's concept of the predatory lover is seen on the rock song "Dirty Diana".[543] The lead single "I Just Can't Stop Loving You" is a traditional love ballad, and "Man in the Mirror" is a ballad of confession and resolution. "[Smooth Criminal](/wiki/Smooth_Criminal "Smooth Criminal")" is an evocation of bloody assault, rape and likely murder.[132] AllMusic's Stephen Thomas Erlewine states that _Dangerous_ presents Jackson as a paradoxical person.[544] The first half of the record is dedicated to new jack swing, including songs like "Jam" and "Remember the Time". It was the first Jackson album in which social ills became a primary theme; "Why You Wanna Trip on Me", for example, protests world hunger, AIDS, homelessness and drugs. _Dangerous_ contains sexually charged songs such as "[In the Closet](/wiki/In_the_Closet "In the Closet")". The title track continues the theme of the predatory lover and compulsive desire. The second half includes introspective, pop-gospel anthems such as "[Will You Be There](/wiki/Will_You_Be_There "Will You Be There")", "Heal the World", and "Keep the Faith".[517] In the ballad "[Gone Too Soon](/wiki/Gone_Too_Soon "Gone Too Soon")", Jackson gives tribute to Ryan White and the plight of those with AIDS.[545]

_HIStory_ creates an atmosphere of paranoia.[546] In the new jack swing-funk rock tracks "Scream" and "Tabloid Junkie", and the R&B ballad "[You Are Not Alone](/wiki/You_Are_Not_Alone "You Are Not Alone")", Jackson retaliates against the injustice and isolation he feels, and directs his anger at the media.[547] In the introspective ballad "[Stranger in Moscow](/wiki/Stranger_in_Moscow "Stranger in Moscow")", Jackson laments his "fall from grace"; "Earth Song", "Childhood", "Little Susie", and "Smile" are operatic pop songs.[546][547] In "[D.S.](/wiki/D.S._\(song\) "D.S. \(song\)")", Jackson attacks lawyer [Thomas W. Sneddon Jr.](/wiki/Thomas_W._Sneddon_Jr. "Thomas W. Sneddon Jr."), who had prosecuted him in both child sexual abuse cases; he describes Sneddon as a white supremacist who wanted to "get my ass, dead or alive".[548] _Invincible_ includes urban soul tracks such as "[Cry](/wiki/Cry_\(Michael_Jackson_song\) "Cry \(Michael Jackson song\)")" and "The Lost Children", ballads such as "[Speechless](/wiki/Speechless_\(Michael_Jackson_song\) "Speechless \(Michael Jackson song\)")", "Break of Dawn", and "Butterflies", and mixes hip-hop, pop, and R&B in "2000 Watts", "Heartbreaker", and "Invincible".[549][550]

### Music videos and choreography

Jackson released "[Thriller](/wiki/Michael_Jackson%27s_Thriller_\(music_video\) "Michael Jackson's Thriller \(music video\)")", a 14-minute music video directed by [John Landis](/wiki/John_Landis "John Landis"), in 1983.[551] The [zombie](/wiki/Zombie "Zombie")-themed video "defined music videos and broke racial barriers" on [MTV](/wiki/MTV "MTV"), which had launched two years earlier.[40] Before _Thriller_ , Jackson struggled to receive coverage on MTV, allegedly because he was African American.[552] Pressure from CBS Records persuaded MTV to start showing "Billie Jean" and later "Beat It", which led to a lengthy partnership with Jackson, and helped other black music artists gain recognition.[553] The popularity of his videos on MTV helped the relatively new channel's viewing figures, and MTV's focus shifted toward pop and R&B.[553][554] His performance on _Motown 25: Yesterday, Today, Forever_ changed the scope of live stage shows, making it acceptable for artists to lip-sync to music video on stage.[555] The choreography in _Thriller_ has been copied in [Indian films](/wiki/Indian_films "Indian films") and [prisons in the Philippines](/wiki/Thriller_\(viral_video\) "Thriller \(viral video\)").[556] _Thriller_ marked an increase in scale for music videos, and was named the most successful music video ever by the _Guinness World Records_.[237]

In the 19-minute video for "Bad"—directed by [Martin Scorsese](/wiki/Martin_Scorsese "Martin Scorsese")—Jackson used sexual imagery and choreography, and touched his chest, torso and crotch. When asked by Winfrey in the 1993 interview about why he grabbed his crotch, he said it was spontaneously compelled by the music. _Time_ magazine described the "Bad" video as "infamous". It featured [Wesley Snipes](/wiki/Wesley_Snipes "Wesley Snipes"); Jackson's later videos often featured famous cameos.[557][558] For the "Smooth Criminal" video, Jackson experimented with leaning forward at a 45-degree angle, beyond the performer's center of gravity. To accomplish this live, Jackson and designers developed a special shoe to lock the performer's feet to the stage. They were granted [U.S. patent 5,255,452](http://patft.uspto.gov/netacgi/nph-Parser?patentnumber=5255452) for the device.[559] The video for "Leave Me Alone" was not officially released in the US, but in 1989 was nominated for three _Billboard_ Music Video Awards[560] and won a Golden Lion Award for its special effects. It won a Grammy for [Best Music Video, Short Form](/wiki/Best_Music_Video,_Short_Form "Best Music Video, Short Form").[57]

He received the [MTV Video Vanguard Award](/wiki/MTV_Video_Vanguard_Award "MTV Video Vanguard Award") in 1988; in 2001 the award was renamed in his honor.[561] The "Black or White" video simultaneously premiered on November 14, 1991, in 27 countries with an estimated audience of 500 million people, the largest audience ever for a music video at the time.[171] Along with Jackson, it featured [Macaulay Culkin](/wiki/Macaulay_Culkin "Macaulay Culkin"), [Peggy Lipton](/wiki/Peggy_Lipton "Peggy Lipton"), and [George Wendt](/wiki/George_Wendt "George Wendt"). It helped introduce [morphing](/wiki/Morphing "Morphing") to music videos.[562] It was controversial for scenes in which Jackson rubs his crotch, vandalizes cars, and throws a garbage can through a storefront. He apologized and removed the final scene of the video.[160]

"In the Closet" featured [Naomi Campbell](/wiki/Naomi_Campbell "Naomi Campbell") in a courtship dance with Jackson.[563] "Remember the Time" was set in [ancient Egypt](/wiki/Ancient_Egypt "Ancient Egypt"), and featured [Eddie Murphy](/wiki/Eddie_Murphy "Eddie Murphy"), [Iman](/wiki/Iman_\(model\) "Iman \(model\)"), and [Magic Johnson](/wiki/Magic_Johnson "Magic Johnson").[564] The video for "Scream", directed by [Mark Romanek](/wiki/Mark_Romanek "Mark Romanek") and production designer Tom Foden, gained a record 11 [MTV Video Music Award](/wiki/MTV_Video_Music_Award "MTV Video Music Award") Nominations, and won "Best Dance Video", "Best Choreography", and "Best Art Direction".[565] The song and its video are Jackson's response to being accused of child molestation in 1993.[566] A year later, it won a Grammy for Best Music Video, Short Form. It has been reported as the [most expensive music video ever made](/wiki/List_of_most_expensive_music_videos "List of most expensive music videos"), at $7 million;[567] Romanek has contradicted this.[568] The "Earth Song" video was nominated for the 1997 Grammy for Best Music Video, Short Form.[569]

_[Michael Jackson's Ghosts](/wiki/Michael_Jackson%27s_Ghosts "Michael Jackson's Ghosts")_ , a short film written by Jackson and [Stephen King](/wiki/Stephen_King "Stephen King") and directed by [Stan Winston](/wiki/Stan_Winston "Stan Winston"), premiered at the 1996 [Cannes Film Festival](/wiki/Cannes_Film_Festival "Cannes Film Festival"). At over 38 minutes, it held the Guinness world record for the longest music video until 2013.[570] The 2001 video for "[You Rock My World](/wiki/You_Rock_My_World "You Rock My World")" lasts over 13 minutes, was directed by [Paul Hunter](/wiki/Paul_Hunter_\(director\) "Paul Hunter \(director\)"), and features [Chris Tucker](/wiki/Chris_Tucker "Chris Tucker") and [Marlon Brando](/wiki/Marlon_Brando "Marlon Brando").[571] It won an [NAACP Image Award](/wiki/NAACP_Image_Award "NAACP Image Award") for Outstanding Music Video in 2002.[572]

In December 2009, the [Library of Congress](/wiki/Library_of_Congress "Library of Congress") selected "Thriller" as the only music video on the [National Film Registry](/wiki/National_Film_Registry "National Film Registry"), as a work of "enduring importance to American culture".[573][574] Huey wrote that Jackson transformed the music video into an artform and a promotional tool through complex story lines, dance routines, special effects and famous cameos, while breaking down racial barriers.[10]

## Honors and awards

See also: [List of awards and nominations received by Michael Jackson](/wiki/List_of_awards_and_nominations_received_by_Michael_Jackson "List of awards and nominations received by Michael Jackson")

[](/wiki/File:George_H._W._Bush_with_Michael_Jackson.png)Jackson and President [George H. W. Bush](/wiki/George_H._W._Bush "George H. W. Bush") at the White House on April 5, 1990. It was the second time that Jackson had been honored by a US president.

Jackson is one of the [best-selling music artists](/wiki/List_of_best-selling_music_artists "List of best-selling music artists") in history,[575] with worldwide record sales estimated from 500 million to more than 1 billion.[576][577][578][Note 2] He had 13 [number-one singles in the US](/wiki/List_of_artists_who_reached_number_one_in_the_United_States#J "List of artists who reached number one in the United States") over his solo career—more than any other male artist in the Hot 100 era at the time of his death.[579] Jackson is one of only three recording artists who have sold over 100 million records both as solo artists and separately as principal members of a band.[580] He was invited to the [White House](/wiki/White_House "White House") three times. In 1984, he was honored with a "Presidential Public Safety Commendation" award by [Ronald Reagan](/wiki/Ronald_Reagan "Ronald Reagan") for his humanitarian endeavors.[581] In 1990, he was honored as the "Artist of the Decade", which was awarded by the [National Children's Museum](/wiki/National_Children%27s_Museum "National Children's Museum"), by [George H. W. Bush](/wiki/George_H._W._Bush "George H. W. Bush").[582] In 1992, he was honored as a "Point of Light Ambassador" by Bush for inviting disadvantaged children to his [Neverland Ranch](/wiki/Neverland_Ranch "Neverland Ranch").[583]

Jackson won hundreds of awards and is one of the most-awarded artists in popular music.[584] His awards include 39 Guinness World Records, including the Most Successful Entertainer of All Time,[464][465] 13 [Grammy Awards](/wiki/Grammy_Award "Grammy Award"),[585] as well as the [Grammy Legend Award](/wiki/Grammy_Legend_Award "Grammy Legend Award")[586] and the [Grammy Lifetime Achievement Award](/wiki/Grammy_Lifetime_Achievement_Award "Grammy Lifetime Achievement Award"),[587] and 26 American Music Awards, including the Artist of the Century and Artist of the 1980s.[259] He received the [World Music Awards](/wiki/World_Music_Awards "World Music Awards")'s Best Selling Male Artist of the Millennium and the [Bambi](/wiki/Bambi_Award "Bambi Award") Pop Artist of the Millennium Award.[260][588]

[](/wiki/File:MJ%27s_star.png)Jackson's star on the [Hollywood Walk of Fame](/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame "Hollywood Walk of Fame") on the occasion of the 15th anniversary of his death

Jackson was inducted onto the [Hollywood Walk of Fame](/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame "Hollywood Walk of Fame") in 1980 as a member of the Jacksons, and in 1984 as a solo artist. He was [inducted to the Rock and Roll Hall of Fame](/wiki/List_of_Rock_and_Roll_Hall_of_Fame_inductees#Performers "List of Rock and Roll Hall of Fame inductees") and [Vocal Group Hall of Fame](/wiki/Vocal_Group_Hall_of_Fame "Vocal Group Hall of Fame") as a member of the Jackson 5 in 1997 and 1999,[589] respectively, and again as a solo artist in 2001.[590] In 2002, he was added to the [Songwriters Hall of Fame](/wiki/Songwriters_Hall_of_Fame "Songwriters Hall of Fame").[591] In 2010, he became the first recording artist to be inducted into the [Dance Hall of Fame](/wiki/National_Museum_of_Dance_and_Hall_of_Fame "National Museum of Dance and Hall of Fame"),[592] and in 2014, he was posthumously inducted into the [Rhythm and Blues Music Hall of Fame](/wiki/Rhythm_and_Blues_Music_Hall_of_Fame "Rhythm and Blues Music Hall of Fame").[593] In 2021, he was among the inaugural inductees into the [Black Music & Entertainment Walk of Fame](/wiki/Black_Music_%26_Entertainment_Walk_of_Fame "Black Music & Entertainment Walk of Fame").[594]

In 1988, [Fisk University](/wiki/Fisk_University "Fisk University") honored him with an honorary doctorate.[595] In 1992, he was invested as a [titular king](/wiki/Title_of_honour "Title of honour") of [Sanwi](/wiki/Sanwi "Sanwi"), a traditional African kingdom.[596] In 2009, the Lunar Republic Society named a crater on the Moon after Jackson.[597] In August, for what would have been Jackson's 51st birthday, Google dedicated their [Google Doodle](/wiki/Google_Doodle "Google Doodle") to him.[598] In 2012, the extinct hermit crab _[Mesoparapylocheles michaeljacksoni](/wiki/Mesoparapylocheles_michaeljacksoni "Mesoparapylocheles michaeljacksoni")_ was named in his honor.[599] In 2014, the [British Council](/wiki/British_Council "British Council") of Cultural Relations deemed Jackson's life one of the 80 most important cultural moments of the 20th century.[600] [World Vitiligo Day](/wiki/World_Vitiligo_Day "World Vitiligo Day") is June 25, the anniversary of Jackson's death, to raise awareness of the auto-immune disorder that Jackson suffered from.[601]

## Earnings

Main article: [Estate of Michael Jackson](/wiki/Estate_of_Michael_Jackson "Estate of Michael Jackson")

In 1989, Jackson's annual earnings from album sales, endorsements, and concerts were estimated at $125 million.[237] _[Forbes](/wiki/Forbes "Forbes")_ placed Jackson's annual income at $35 million in 1996 and $20 million in 1997.[602] Jackson has been one of the wealthiest [celebrities](/wiki/List_of_celebrities_by_net_worth "List of celebrities by net worth") and [musical artists](/wiki/List_of_music_artists_by_net_worth "List of music artists by net worth"); estimates of Jackson's net worth during his life range from negative $285 million to positive $350 million for 2002, 2003 and 2007.[603][604] _Forbes_ reported in August 2018 that Jackson's total career pretax earnings in life and death were $4.2 billion.[605][606] Sales of his recordings through Sony's music unit earned him an estimated $300 million in royalties. He may have earned another $400 million from concerts, music publishing (including his share of the Beatles catalog), endorsements, merchandising and music videos.[607]

In 2013, the executors of Jackson's estate filed a petition in the [United States Tax Court](/wiki/United_States_Tax_Court "United States Tax Court") as a result of a dispute with the [Internal Revenue Service](/wiki/Internal_Revenue_Service "Internal Revenue Service") (IRS) over [estate taxes](/wiki/Estate_tax_in_the_United_States "Estate tax in the United States").[608] The executors valued it at about $7 million, while the IRS valued it at more than $1.1 billion. In February 2014, the IRS reported that Jackson's estate owed $505 million in taxes, and $197 million in penalties.[609] In 2021, the Tax Court issued a ruling in favor of the estate, ruling that the estate's total combined value was $111.5 million and that the value of Jackson's name and likeness was $4 million.[610]

In 2016, _Forbes_ estimated annual gross earnings by the Jackson estate at $825 million, the largest ever for a celebrity, mostly due to the sale of the Sony/ATV catalog.[611] In 2018, the figure was $400 million,[612] and by 2024, it rose to $600 million,[613] bringing Jackson's postmortem total to $3.3 billion.[613] _Forbes_ has consistently recognized Jackson as one of the [top-earning dead celebrities](/wiki/Forbes%27_list_of_the_world%27s_highest-paid_dead_celebrity "Forbes' list of the world's highest-paid dead celebrity"), placing him at the top spot from 2010 to 2024, except for 2012, 2021, and 2022.[614][615]

Witnesses do not discuss spiritual matters with a person who has disassociated himself from the Witnesses, including family members.   * "[You Light Up My Life](/wiki/You_Light_Up_My_Life_\(song\) "You Light Up My Life \(song\)")" – [Debby Boone](/wiki/Debby_Boone "Debby Boone") (1978)

Between 1996–2015, the category was retired.

1970s| 

  * "[Superstition](/wiki/Superstition_\(song\) "Superstition \(song\)")" – [Stevie Wonder](/wiki/Stevie_Wonder "Stevie Wonder") (1974)
  * "[Midnight Train to Georgia](/wiki/Midnight_Train_to_Georgia "Midnight Train to Georgia")" – [Gladys Knight & the Pips](/wiki/Gladys_Knight_%26_the_Pips "Gladys Knight & the Pips") (1975)
  * "[Get Down Tonight](/wiki/Get_Down_Tonight "Get Down Tonight")" – [KC and the Sunshine Band](/wiki/KC_and_the_Sunshine_Band "KC and the Sunshine Band") (1976)
  * "[You'll Never Find Another Love Like Mine](/wiki/You%27ll_Never_Find_Another_Love_Like_Mine "You'll Never Find Another Love Like Mine")" – [Lou Rawls](/wiki/Lou_Rawls "Lou Rawls") / "[Play That Funky Music](/wiki/Play_That_Funky_Music "Play That Funky Music")" – [Wild Cherry](/wiki/Wild_Cherry_\(band\) "Wild Cherry \(band\)") (1977)
  * "[Best of My Love](/wiki/Best_of_My_Love_\(The_Emotions_song\) "Best of My Love \(The Emotions song\)")" – [The Emotions](/wiki/The_Emotions "The Emotions") (1978)
  * "[Too Much, Too Little, Too Late](/wiki/Too_Much,_Too_Little,_Too_Late "Too Much, Too Little, Too Late")" – [Johnny Mathis](/wiki/Johnny_Mathis "Johnny Mathis") and [Deniece Williams](/wiki/Deniece_Williams "Deniece Williams") (1979)

  
1980s| 

  * "[Don't Stop 'Til You Get Enough](/wiki/Don%27t_Stop_%27Til_You_Get_Enough "Don't Stop 'Til You Get Enough")" – Michael Jackson (1980)
  * "[Upside Down](/wiki/Upside_Down_\(Diana_Ross_song\) "Upside Down \(Diana Ross song\)")" – [Diana Ross](/wiki/Diana_Ross "Diana Ross") (1981)
  * "[Endless Love](/wiki/Endless_Love_\(song\) "Endless Love \(song\)")" – [Lionel Richie](/wiki/Lionel_Richie "Lionel Richie") and [Diana Ross](/wiki/Diana_Ross "Diana Ross") (1982)
  * "[Sexual Healing](/wiki/Sexual_Healing "Sexual Healing")" – [Marvin Gaye](/wiki/Marvin_Gaye "Marvin Gaye") (1983)
  * "[All Night Long (All Night)](/wiki/All_Night_Long_\(All_Night\) "All Night Long \(All Night\)")" – [Lionel Richie](/wiki/Lionel_Richie "Lionel Richie") (1984)
  * "[When Doves Cry](/wiki/When_Doves_Cry "When Doves Cry")" – [Prince](/wiki/Prince_\(musician\) "Prince \(musician\)") (1985)
  * "[You Give Good Love](/wiki/You_Give_Good_Love "You Give Good Love")" – [Whitney Houston](/wiki/Whitney_Houston "Whitney Houston") (1986)
  * "[Nasty](/wiki/Nasty_\(Janet_Jackson_song\) "Nasty \(Janet Jackson song\)")" – [Janet Jackson](/wiki/Janet_Jackson "Janet Jackson") (1987)
  * "[Bad](/wiki/Bad_\(Michael_Jackson_song\) "Bad \(Michael Jackson song\)")" – Michael Jackson (1988)
  * "[Nice 'n' Slow](/wiki/Nice_%27n%27_Slow "Nice 'n' Slow")" – [Freddie Jackson](/wiki/Freddie_Jackson "Freddie Jackson") (1989)

  
1990s| 

  * "[Miss You Much](/wiki/Miss_You_Much "Miss You Much")" – [Janet Jackson](/wiki/Janet_Jackson "Janet Jackson") (1990)
  * "[U Can't Touch This](/wiki/U_Can%27t_Touch_This "U Can't Touch This")" – [MC Hammer](/wiki/MC_Hammer "MC Hammer") (1991)
  * "[I Wanna Sex You Up](/wiki/I_Wanna_Sex_You_Up "I Wanna Sex You Up")" – [Color Me Badd](/wiki/Color_Me_Badd "Color Me Badd") (1992)
  * "[Remember the Time](/wiki/Remember_the_Time "Remember the Time")" – Michael Jackson (1993)
  * "[I Will Always Love You](/wiki/I_Will_Always_Love_You "I Will Always Love You")" – [Whitney Houston](/wiki/Whitney_Houston "Whitney Houston") (1994)
  * "[I'll Make Love to You](/wiki/I%27ll_Make_Love_to_You "I'll Make Love to You")" – [Boyz II Men](/wiki/Boyz_II_Men "Boyz II Men") (1995)

  
2010s| 

  * "[Work](/wiki/Work_\(Rihanna_song\) "Work \(Rihanna song\)")" – [Rihanna](/wiki/Rihanna "Rihanna") featuring [Drake](/wiki/Drake_\(musician\) "Drake \(musician\)") (2016)
  * "[That's What I Like](/wiki/That%27s_What_I_Like_\(Bruno_Mars_song\) "That's What I Like \(Bruno Mars song\)")" – [Bruno Mars](/wiki/Bruno_Mars "Bruno Mars") (2017)
  * "[Finesse (Remix)](/wiki/Finesse_\(Bruno_Mars_song\) "Finesse \(Bruno Mars song\)")" – [Bruno Mars](/wiki/Bruno_Mars "Bruno Mars") featuring [Cardi B](/wiki/Cardi_B "Cardi B") (2018)
  * "[Talk](/wiki/Talk_\(Khalid_song\) "Talk \(Khalid song\)")" – [Khalid](/wiki/Khalid_\(American_singer\) "Khalid \(American singer\)") (2019)

  
2020s| 

  * "[Heartless](/wiki/Heartless_\(The_Weeknd_song\) "Heartless \(The Weeknd song\)")" – [The Weeknd](/wiki/The_Weeknd "The Weeknd") (2020)
  * "[Leave the Door Open](/wiki/Leave_the_Door_Open "Leave the Door Open")" – [Silk Sonic](/wiki/Silk_Sonic "Silk Sonic") ([Bruno Mars](/wiki/Bruno_Mars "Bruno Mars") & [Anderson .Paak](/wiki/Anderson_.Paak "Anderson .Paak")) (2021)
  * "[Essence](/wiki/Essence_\(Wizkid_song\) "Essence \(Wizkid song\)")" – [Wizkid](/wiki/Wizkid "Wizkid") featuring [Tems](/wiki/Tems_\(singer\) "Tems \(singer\)") (2022)
  * "[Saturn](/wiki/Saturn_\(SZA_song\) "Saturn \(SZA song\)")" – [SZA](/wiki/SZA "SZA") (2025)

  
Between 1996–2015, the category was retired.

1950s| 

  * "[Nel blu, dipinto di blu](/wiki/Nel_blu,_dipinto_di_blu "Nel blu, dipinto di blu")" – [Domenico Modugno](/wiki/Domenico_Modugno "Domenico Modugno") (May 1959)
  * "[Mack the Knife](/wiki/Mack_the_Knife "Mack the Knife")" – [Bobby Darin](/wiki/Bobby_Darin "Bobby Darin") (November 1959)

  
1960s| 

  * "[Theme from _A Summer Place_](/wiki/Theme_from_A_Summer_Place "Theme from A Summer Place")" – [Percy Faith](/wiki/Percy_Faith "Percy Faith") (1961)
  * "[Moon River](/wiki/Moon_River "Moon River")" – [Henry Mancini](/wiki/Henry_Mancini "Henry Mancini") (1962)
  * "[I Left My Heart in San Francisco](/wiki/I_Left_My_Heart_in_San_Francisco "I Left My Heart in San Francisco")" – [Tony Bennett](/wiki/Tony_Bennett "Tony Bennett") (1963)
  * "[Days of Wine and Roses](/wiki/Days_of_Wine_and_Roses_\(song\) "Days of Wine and Roses \(song\)")" – [Henry Mancini](/wiki/Henry_Mancini "Henry Mancini") (1964)
  * "[The Girl from Ipanema](/wiki/The_Girl_from_Ipanema "The Girl from Ipanema")" – [Astrud Gilberto](/wiki/Astrud_Gilberto "Astrud Gilberto") & [Stan Getz](/wiki/Stan_Getz "Stan Getz") (1965)
  * "[A Taste of Honey](/wiki/A_Taste_of_Honey_\(song\) "A Taste of Honey \(song\)")" – [Herb Alpert](/wiki/Herb_Alpert "Herb Alpert") & the Tijuana Brass (1966)
  * "[Strangers in the Night](/wiki/Strangers_in_the_Night "Strangers in the Night")" – [Frank Sinatra](/wiki/Frank_Sinatra "Frank Sinatra") (1967)
  * "[Up, Up and Away](/wiki/Up,_Up_and_Away_\(song\) "Up, Up and Away \(song\)")" – [The 5th Dimension](/wiki/The_5th_Dimension "The 5th Dimension") (1968)
  * "[Mrs. Robinson](/wiki/Mrs._Robinson "Mrs. Robinson")" – [Simon & Garfunkel](/wiki/Simon_%26_Garfunkel "Simon & Garfunkel") (1969)

  
1970s| 

  * "[Aquarius/Let the Sunshine In](/wiki/Aquarius/Let_the_Sunshine_In "Aquarius/Let the Sunshine In")" – [The 5th Dimension](/wiki/The_5th_Dimension "The 5th Dimension") (1970)
  * "[Bridge over Troubled Water](/wiki/Bridge_over_Troubled_Water_\(song\) "Bridge over Troubled Water \(song\)")" – [Simon & Garfunkel](/wiki/Simon_%26_Garfunkel "Simon & Garfunkel") (1971)
  * "[It's Too Late](/wiki/It%27s_Too_Late_\(Carole_King_song\) "It's Too Late \(Carole King song\)")" – [Carole King](/wiki/Carole_King "Carole King") (1972)
  * "[The First Time Ever I Saw Your Face](/wiki/The_First_Time_Ever_I_Saw_Your_Face "The First Time Ever I Saw Your Face")" – [Roberta Flack](/wiki/Roberta_Flack "Roberta Flack") (1973)
  * "[Killing Me Softly with His Song](/wiki/Killing_Me_Softly_with_His_Song "Killing Me Softly with His Song")" – [Roberta Flack](/wiki/Roberta_Flack "Roberta Flack") (1974)
  * "[I Honestly Love You](/wiki/I_Honestly_Love_You "I Honestly Love You")" – [Olivia Newton-John](/wiki/Olivia_Newton-John "Olivia Newton-John") (1975)
  * "[Love Will Keep Us Together](/wiki/Love_Will_Keep_Us_Together "Love Will Keep Us Together")" – [Captain & Tennille](/wiki/Captain_%26_Tennille "Captain & Tennille") (1976)
  * "[This Masquerade](/wiki/This_Masquerade "This Masquerade")" – [George Benson](/wiki/George_Benson "George Benson") (1977)
  * "[Hotel California](/wiki/Hotel_California "Hotel California")" – [Eagles](/wiki/Eagles_\(band\) "Eagles \(band\)") (1978)
  * "[Just the Way You Are](/wiki/Just_the_Way_You_Are_\(Billy_Joel_song\) "Just the Way You Are \(Billy Joel song\)")" – [Billy Joel](/wiki/Billy_Joel "Billy Joel") (1979)

  
1980s| 

  * "[What a Fool Believes](/wiki/What_a_Fool_Believes "What a Fool Believes")" – [The Doobie Brothers](/wiki/The_Doobie_Brothers "The Doobie Brothers") (1980)
  * "[Sailing](/wiki/Sailing_\(Christopher_Cross_song\) "Sailing \(Christopher Cross song\)")" – [Christopher Cross](/wiki/Christopher_Cross "Christopher Cross") (1981)
  * "[Bette Davis Eyes](/wiki/Bette_Davis_Eyes "Bette Davis Eyes")" – [Kim Carnes](/wiki/Kim_Carnes "Kim Carnes") (1982)
  * "[Rosanna](/wiki/Rosanna_\(song\) "Rosanna \(song\)")" – [Toto](/wiki/Toto_\(band\) "Toto \(band\)") (1983)
  * "[Beat It](/wiki/Beat_It "Beat It")" – Michael Jackson (1984)
  * "[What's Love Got to Do with It](/wiki/What%27s_Love_Got_to_Do_with_It_\(song\) "What's Love Got to Do with It \(song\)")" – [Tina Turner](/wiki/Tina_Turner "Tina Turner") (1985)
  * "[We Are the World](/wiki/We_Are_the_World "We Are the World")" – [USA for Africa](/wiki/USA_for_Africa "USA for Africa") (1986)

  * "[Don't Worry, Be Happy](/wiki/Don%27t_Worry,_Be_Happy "Don't Worry, Be Happy")" – [Bobby McFerrin](/wiki/Bobby_McFerrin "Bobby McFerrin") (1989)

  
1990s|

  * "[Unforgettable](/wiki/Unforgettable_\(Nat_King_Cole_song\) "Unforgettable \(Nat King Cole song\)")" – [Natalie Cole](/wiki/Natalie_Cole "Natalie Cole") with [Nat King Cole](/wiki/Nat_King_Cole "Nat King Cole") (1992)
  * "[Tears in Heaven](/wiki/Tears_in_Heaven "Tears in Heaven")" – [Eric Clapton](/wiki/Eric_Clapton "Eric Clapton") (1993)
  * "[I Will Always Love You](/wiki/I_Will_Always_Love_You_\(Whitney_Houston_recording\) "I Will Always Love You \(Whitney Houston recording\)")" – [Whitney Houston](/wiki/Whitney_Houston "Whitney Houston") (1994)
  * "[All I Wanna Do](/wiki/All_I_Wanna_Do_\(Sheryl_Crow_song\) "All I Wanna Do \(Sheryl Crow song\)")" – [Sheryl Crow](/wiki/Sheryl_Crow "Sheryl Crow") (1995)

  * "[Change the World](/wiki/Change_the_World "Change the World")" – [Eric Clapton](/wiki/Eric_Clapton "Eric Clapton") (1997)

2000s|

1980s| 

  * "[What a Fool Believes](/wiki/What_a_Fool_Believes "What a Fool Believes")" – [Kenny Loggins](/wiki/Kenny_Loggins "Kenny Loggins") & [Michael McDonald](/wiki/Michael_McDonald_\(musician\) "Michael McDonald \(musician\)") (1980)

  * "[Somewhere Out There](/wiki/Somewhere_Out_There_\(An_American_Tail_song\) "Somewhere Out There \(An American Tail song\)")" – [James Horner](/wiki/James_Horner "James Horner"), [Barry Mann](/wiki/Barry_Mann "Barry Mann") & [Cynthia Weil](/wiki/Cynthia_Weil "Cynthia Weil") (1988)
  * "[Don't Worry, Be Happy](/wiki/Don%27t_Worry,_Be_Happy "Don't Worry, Be Happy")" – [Bobby McFerrin](/wiki/Bobby_McFerrin "Bobby McFerrin") (1989)

  
1990s| 

  * "[Wind Beneath My Wings](/wiki/Wind_Beneath_My_Wings "Wind Beneath My Wings")" – [Larry Henley](/wiki/Larry_Henley "Larry Henley") & [Jeff Silbar](/wiki/Jeff_Silbar "Jeff Silbar") (1990)
  * "[From a Distance](/wiki/From_a_Distance "From a Distance")" – [Julie Gold](/wiki/Julie_Gold "Julie Gold") (1991)
  * "[Unforgettable](/wiki/Unforgettable_\(Nat_King_Cole_song\) "Unforgettable \(Nat King Cole song\)")" – [Irving Gordon](/wiki/Irving_Gordon "Irving Gordon") (1992)
  * "[Tears in Heaven](/wiki/Tears_in_Heaven "Tears in Heaven")" – [Eric Clapton](/wiki/Eric_Clapton "Eric Clapton") & [Will Jennings](/wiki/Will_Jennings "Will Jennings") (1993)
  * "[A Whole New World](/wiki/A_Whole_New_World "A Whole New World")" – [Alan Menken](/wiki/Alan_Menken "Alan Menken") & [Tim Rice](/wiki/Tim_Rice "Tim Rice") (1994)
  * "[Streets of Philadelphia](/wiki/Streets_of_Philadelphia "Streets of Philadelphia")" – [Bruce Springsteen](/wiki/Bruce_Springsteen "Bruce Springsteen") (1995)
  * "[Kiss from a Rose](/wiki/Kiss_from_a_Rose "Kiss from a Rose")" – [Seal](/wiki/Seal_\(musician\) "Seal \(musician\)") (1996)
  * "[Change the World](/wiki/Change_the_World "Change the World")" – [Gordon Kennedy](/wiki/Gordon_Kennedy_\(musician\) "Gordon Kennedy \(musician\)"), [Wayne Kirkpatrick](/wiki/Wayne_Kirkpatrick "Wayne Kirkpatrick") & [Tommy Sims](/wiki/Tommy_Sims "Tommy Sims") (1997)
  * "[Sunny Came Home](/wiki/Sunny_Came_Home "Sunny Came Home")" – [Shawn Colvin](/wiki/Shawn_Colvin "Shawn Colvin") & [John Leventhal](/wiki/John_Leventhal "John Leventhal") (1998)
  * "[My Heart Will Go On](/wiki/My_Heart_Will_Go_On "My Heart Will Go On")" – [James Horner](/wiki/James_Horner "James Horner") & [Will Jennings](/wiki/Will_Jennings "Will Jennings") (1999)

  
2000s| 

  * "[Smooth](/wiki/Smooth_\(Santana_song\) "Smooth \(Santana song\)")" – [Itaal Shur](/wiki/Itaal_Shur "Itaal Shur") & [Rob Thomas](/wiki/Rob_Thomas_\(musician\) "Rob Thomas \(musician\)") (2000)
  * "[Beautiful Day](/wiki/Beautiful_Day "Beautiful Day")" – [Adam Clayton](/wiki/Adam_Clayton "Adam Clayton"), [David Evans](/wiki/The_Edge "The Edge"), [Laurence Mullen](/wiki/Larry_Mullen_Jr. "Larry Mullen Jr.") & [Paul Hewson](/wiki/Bono "Bono") (2001)
  * "[Fallin'](/wiki/Fallin%27_\(Alicia_Keys_song\) "Fallin' \(Alicia Keys song\)")" – [Alicia Keys](/wiki/Alicia_Keys "Alicia Keys") (2002)
  * "[Don't Know Why](/wiki/Don%27t_Know_Why "Don't Know Why")" – [Jesse Harris](/wiki/Jesse_Harris "Jesse Harris") (2003)
  * "[Dance with My Father](/wiki/Dance_with_My_Father_\(song\) "Dance with My Father \(song\)")" – [Richard Marx](/wiki/Richard_Marx "Richard Marx") & [Luther Vandross](/wiki/Luther_Vandross "Luther Vandross") (2004)
  * "[Daughters](/wiki/Daughters_\(John_Mayer_song\) "Daughters \(John Mayer song\)")" – [John Mayer](/wiki/John_Mayer "John Mayer") (2005)
  * "[Sometimes You Can't Make It on Your Own](/wiki/Sometimes_You_Can%27t_Make_It_on_Your_Own "Sometimes You Can't Make It on Your Own")" – [Adam Clayton](/wiki/Adam_Clayton "Adam Clayton"), [David Evans](/wiki/The_Edge "The Edge"), [Laurence Mullen](/wiki/Larry_Mullen_Jr. "Larry Mullen Jr.") & [Paul Hewson](/wiki/Bono "Bono") (2006)
  * "[Not Ready to Make Nice](/wiki/Not_Ready_to_Make_Nice "Not Ready to Make Nice")" – [Emily Burns Erwin](/wiki/Emily_Robison "Emily Robison"), [Martha Maguire](/wiki/Martie_Maguire "Martie Maguire"), [Natalie Maines Pasdar](/wiki/Natalie_Maines "Natalie Maines") & [Dan Wilson](/wiki/Dan_Wilson_\(musician\) "Dan Wilson \(musician\)") (2007)
  * "[Rehab](/wiki/Rehab_\(Amy_Winehouse_song\) "Rehab \(Amy Winehouse song\)")" – [Amy Winehouse](/wiki/Amy_Winehouse "Amy Winehouse") (2008)
  * "[Viva la Vida](/wiki/Viva_la_Vida "Viva la Vida")" – [Guy Berryman](/wiki/Guy_Berryman "Guy Berryman"), [Jonathan Buckland](/wiki/Jonny_Buckland "Jonny Buckland"), [William Champion](/wiki/Will_Champion "Will Champion") & [Christopher Martin](/wiki/Chris_Martin "Chris Martin") (2009)

  
2010s| 

  * "[Single Ladies (Put a Ring on It)](/wiki/Single_Ladies_\(Put_a_Ring_on_It\) "Single Ladies \(Put a Ring on It\)")" – [Thaddis "Kuk" Harrell](/wiki/Kuk_Harrell "Kuk Harrell"), [Beyoncé Knowles](/wiki/Beyonc%C3%A9 "Beyoncé"), [Terius Nash](/wiki/The-Dream "The-Dream") & [Christopher Stewart](/wiki/Tricky_Stewart "Tricky Stewart") (2010)
  * "[Need You Now](/wiki/Need_You_Now_\(Lady_Antebellum_song\) "Need You Now \(Lady Antebellum song\)")" – [Dave Haywood](/wiki/Dave_Haywood "Dave Haywood"), [Josh Kear](/wiki/Josh_Kear "Josh Kear"), [Charles Kelley](/wiki/Charles_Kelley "Charles Kelley") & [Hillary Scott](/wiki/Hillary_Scott "Hillary Scott") (2011)
  * "[Rolling in the Deep](/wiki/Rolling_in_the_Deep "Rolling in the Deep")" – [Adele Adkins](/wiki/Adele "Adele") & [Paul Epworth](/wiki/Paul_Epworth "Paul Epworth") (2012)
  * "[We Are Young](/wiki/We_Are_Young "We Are Young")" – [Jack Antonoff](/wiki/Jack_Antonoff "Jack Antonoff"), [Jeff Bhasker](/wiki/Jeff_Bhasker "Jeff Bhasker"), [Andrew Dost](/wiki/Andrew_Dost "Andrew Dost") & [Nate Ruess](/wiki/Nate_Ruess "Nate Ruess") (2013)
  * "[Royals](/wiki/Royals_\(song\) "Royals \(song\)")" – [Joel Little](/wiki/Joel_Little "Joel Little") & [Ella Yelich O'Connor](/wiki/Lorde "Lorde") (2014)
  * "[Stay with Me](/wiki/Stay_with_Me_\(Sam_Smith_song\) "Stay with Me \(Sam Smith song\)")" ([Darkchild](/wiki/Rodney_Jerkins "Rodney Jerkins") version) – [James Napier](/wiki/Jimmy_Napes "Jimmy Napes"), [William Phillips](/wiki/Tourist_\(musician\) "Tourist \(musician\)") & [Sam Smith](/wiki/Sam_Smith "Sam Smith") (2015)
  * "[Thinking Out Loud](/wiki/Thinking_Out_Loud "Thinking Out Loud")" – [Ed Sheeran](/wiki/Ed_Sheeran "Ed Sheeran") & [Amy Wadge](/wiki/Amy_Wadge "Amy Wadge") (2016)
  * "[Hello](/wiki/Hello_\(Adele_song\) "Hello \(Adele song\)")" – [Adele Adkins](/wiki/Adele "Adele") & [Greg Kurstin](/wiki/Greg_Kurstin "Greg Kurstin") (2017)
  * "[That's What I Like](/wiki/That%27s_What_I_Like_\(Bruno_Mars_song\) "That's What I Like \(Bruno Mars song\)")" – [Christopher Brody Brown](/wiki/Brody_Brown "Brody Brown"), [James Fauntleroy](/wiki/James_Fauntleroy "James Fauntleroy"), [Philip Lawrence](/wiki/Philip_Lawrence_\(songwriter\) "Philip Lawrence \(songwriter\)"), [Bruno Mars](/wiki/Bruno_Mars "Bruno Mars"), [Ray Charles McCullough II, Jeremy Reeves, Ray Romulus & Jonathan Yip](/wiki/The_Stereotypes "The Stereotypes") (2018)
  * "[This Is America](/wiki/This_Is_America_\(song\) "This Is America \(song\)")" – [Donald Glover](/wiki/Donald_Glover "Donald Glover"), [Ludwig Göransson](/wiki/Ludwig_G%C3%B6ransson "Ludwig Göransson") & [Jeffery Lamar Williams](/wiki/Young_Thug "Young Thug") (2019)

  
2020s| 

  * "[Bad Guy](/wiki/Bad_Guy_\(Billie_Eilish_song\) "Bad Guy \(Billie Eilish song\)")" – [Billie Eilish O'Connell](/wiki/Billie_Eilish "Billie Eilish") & [Finneas O'Connell](/wiki/Finneas_O%27Connell "Finneas O'Connell") (2020)

  * "[Leave the Door Open](/wiki/Leave_the_Door_Open "Leave the Door Open")" – [Brandon Anderson](/wiki/Anderson_.Paak "Anderson .Paak"), [Christopher Brody Brown](/wiki/Brody_Brown "Brody Brown"), [Dernst Emile II](/wiki/D%27Mile "D'Mile") & [Bruno Mars](/wiki/Bruno_Mars "Bruno Mars") (2022)
